# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

5. Return result.

Related Classes: CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType

Output: List<FundingGroupType> (A list of funding group types)
Pre-condition: None

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary: Calculates the sum of the budget amounts of all projects across all departments in the company.
Algorithm: 
1. Initialize total = 0.0.
2. For each department in departments:
3.   For each project in department.projects:
4.     total += project.budget.
5. Return total.
Related Features: departments, Department::projects
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input: None
Output: double (The total budget sum)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The total budget of all projects is returned.

Company::addDepartment
Summary: Adds a new department to the company while maintaining the constraint of 2-8 departments.
Algorithm: 
1. Check if department is null or name is empty/duplicate; if so, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Check if current departments size < 8; if so, add department.
3. If size >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department
Input: department : Department (The department object to be added)
Output: void
Pre-condition: Current number of departments is less than 8.
Post-condition: The department is added to the departments list; current size is incremented.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary: Counts the number of distinct employees who work on at least one production project.
Algorithm: 
1. Initialize a set of distinct employee IDs.
2. For each department in departments:
3.   For each project in department.projects:
4.     If project is an instance of ProductionProject:
5.       For each employee in project.workingEmployees:
6.         Add employee.ID to the set.
7. Return the size of the set.
Related Features: departments, Department::projects, Project::workingEmployees
Related Classes: Department, Employee, ProductionProject
Input: None
Output: int (The count of distinct employees)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The number of unique employees in production projects is returned.

Company::removeDepartment
Summary: Removes an existing department by name while maintaining the constraint of 2-8 departments.
Algorithm: 
1. If name is null/empty or department not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Check if current departments size > 2; if so, find and remove department.
3. If size <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department
Input: name : String (The name of the department to remove)
Output: String (The ID/name of the removed department)
Pre-condition: Current number of departments is greater than 2.
Post-condition: The department is removed from the company; current size is decremented.

```result
 */

/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...
```

```plantuml
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary: Calculates the sum of the budget amounts of all projects across all departments in the company.
Algorithm: 
1. Initialize total = 0.0.
2. For each department in departments:
3.   For each project in department.projects:
4.     total += project.budget.
5. Return total.
Related Features: departments, Department::projects
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input: None
Output: double (The total budget sum)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The total budget of all projects is returned.

Company::addDepartment
Summary: Adds a new department to the company while maintaining the constraint of 2-8 departments.
Algorithm: 
1. Check if department is null or name is empty/duplicate; if so, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Check if current departments size < 8; if so, add department.
3. If size >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department
Input: department : Department (The department object to be added)
Output: void
Pre-condition: Current number of departments is less than 8.
Post-condition: The department is added to the departments list; current size is incremented.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary: Counts the number of distinct employees who work on at least one production project.
Algorithm: 
1. Initialize a set of distinct employee IDs.
2. For each department in departments:
3.   For each project in department.projects:
4.     If project is an instance of ProductionProject:
5.       For each employee in project.workingEmployees:
6.         Add employee.ID to the set.
7. Return the size of the set.
Related Features: departments, Department::projects, Project::workingEmployees
Related Classes: Department, Employee, ProductionProject
Input: None
Output: int (The count of distinct employees)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The number of unique employees in production projects is returned.

Company::removeDepartment
Summary: Removes an existing department by name while maintaining the constraint of 2-8 departments.
Algorithm: 
1. If name is null/empty or department not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Check if current departments size > 2; if so, find and remove department.
3. If size <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department
Input: name : String (The name of the department to remove)
Output: String (The ID/name of the removed department)
Pre-condition: Current number of departments is greater than 2.
Post-condition: The department is removed from the company; current size is decremented.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.

    for (Project project : getProjects()) {
        if (project instanceof CommunityProject) {
            CommunityProject communityProject = (CommunityProject) project;
            FundingGroup fundingGroup = communityProject.getFundingGroup();
            if (fundingGroup != null && fundingGroup.getType() != null) {
                result.add(fundingGroup.getType());
            }
        }
    }
    return result;
  }

  @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
  @Override
  public NotificationChain eInverseAdd(InternalEObject otherEnd, int featureID, NotificationChain msgs)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return ((InternalEList<InternalEObject>)(InternalEList<?>)getEmployees()).basicAdd(otherEnd, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        if (eInternalContainer() != null)
          msgs = eBasicRemoveFromContainer(msgs);
        return basicSetCompany((Company)otherEnd, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return ((InternalEList<InternalEObject>)(InternalEList<?>)getProjects()).basicAdd(otherEnd, msgs);
    }
    return super.eInverseAdd(otherEnd, featureID, msgs);
  }

  @Override
  public NotificationChain eInverseRemove(InternalEObject otherEnd, int featureID, NotificationChain msgs)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return ((InternalEList<?>)getEmployees()).basicRemove(otherEnd, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return basicSetCompany(null, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return ((InternalEList<?>)getProjects()).basicRemove(otherEnd, msgs);
    }
    return super.eInverseRemove(otherEnd, featureID, msgs);
  }

  @Override
  public NotificationChain eBasicRemoveFromContainerFeature(NotificationChain msgs)
  {
    switch (eContainerFeatureID())
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return eInternalContainer().eInverseRemove(this, ProjectPackage.COMPANY__DEPARTMENTS, Company.class, msgs);
    }
    return super.eBasicRemoveFromContainerFeature(msgs);
  }

  @Override
  public Object eGet(int featureID, boolean resolve, boolean coreType)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        return getID();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        return getEmail();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return getEmployees();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return getCompany();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return getProjects();
    }
    return super.eGet(featureID, resolve, coreType);
  }

  @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
  @Override
  public void eSet(int featureID, Object newValue)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        setID((String)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        setEmail((String)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        getEmployees().clear();
        getEmployees().addAll((Collection<? extends Employee>)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        setCompany((Company)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        getProjects().clear();
        getProjects().addAll((Collection<? extends Project>)newValue);
        return;
    }
    super.eSet(featureID, newValue);
  }

  @Override
  public void eUnset(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        setID(ID_EDEFAULT);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        setEmail(EMAIL_EDEFAULT);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        getEmployees().clear();
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        setCompany((Company)null);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        getProjects().clear();
        return;
    }
    super.eUnset(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public boolean eIsSet(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        return ID_EDEFAULT == null ? id != null : !ID_EDEFAULT.equals(id);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        return EMAIL_EDEFAULT == null ? email != null : !EMAIL_EDEFAULT.equals(email);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return employees != null && !employees.isEmpty();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return getCompany() != null;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return projects != null && !projects.isEmpty();
    }
    return super.eIsSet(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString()
  {
    if (eIsProxy()) return super.toString();

    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder(super.toString());
    result.append(" (id: ");
    result.append(id);
    result.append(", email: ");
    result.append(email);
    result.append(')');
    return result.toString();
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary: Calculates the sum of the budget amounts of all projects across all departments.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize total = 0.
2. For each department in departments:
3.   For each project in department.projects:
4.     total += project.budget.
5. Return total.
Related Features: departments, Department::projects, Project::budget
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input: None
Output: total budget (double)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all project budgets.

Company::addDepartment
Summary: Adds a new department to the company while ensuring the department count constraint (2-8) and uniqueness.
Algorithm: 
1. Check if department is null, name is empty, or duplicate; if so, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. If departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
3. Add department to the departments list.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department
Input: department (Department object)
Output: None
Pre-condition: Current number of departments < 8.
Post-condition: Number of departments increases by 1; department is in the list.

Company::removeDepartment
Summary: Removes an existing department by name while ensuring the department count constraint (2-8).
Algorithm:
1. If name is null/empty or no department found with name, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. If departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
3. Remove department from departments list.
4. Return the name of the removed department.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department
Input: name (String)
Output: name of removed department (String)
Pre-condition: Current number of departments > 2.
Post-condition: Number of departments decreases by 1; department is removed.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production project.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize a Set of Employee objects.
2. For each department in departments:
3.   For each project in department.projects:
4.     If project is instance of ProductionProject:
5.       For each employee in project.workingEmployees:
6.         Add employee to the set.
7. Return set.size().
Related Features: departments, Department::projects, Project::workingEmployees
Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input: None
Output: count (int)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the count of unique employees.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
Summary: Calculates the average budget amount of all projects in this department.
Algorithm:
1. If projects is empty, return 0.0.
2. Sum = sum of all project.budget in projects.
3. Return Sum / projects.size().
Related Features: projects, Project::budget
Related Classes: Project
Input: None
Output: average budget (double)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the average budget value.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
Summary: Retrieves the funding group type of all community projects within this department.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize List of FundingGroupType results.
2. For each project in projects:
3.   If project is instance of CommunityProject:
4.     Add project.fundingGroup.type to results.
5. Return results.
Related Features: projects, CommunityProject::fundingGroup, FundingGroup::type
Related Classes: Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
Input: None
Output: list of FundingGroupType (List<FundingGroupType>)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns a list of all funding group types associated with community projects.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.

  3.3 You should not change the method signature;

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return ((InternalEList<InternalEObject>)(InternalEList<?>)getEmployees()).basicAdd(otherEnd, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        if (eInternalContainer() != null)
          msgs = eBasicRemoveFromContainer(msgs);
        return basicSetCompany((Company)otherEnd, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return ((InternalEList<InternalEObject>)(InternalEList<?>)getProjects()).basicAdd(otherEnd, msgs);
    }
    return super.eInverseAdd(otherEnd, featureID, msgs);
  }

  @Override
  public NotificationChain eInverseRemove(InternalEObject otherEnd, int featureID, NotificationChain msgs)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return ((InternalEList<?>)getEmployees()).basicRemove(otherEnd, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return basicSetCompany(null, msgs);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return ((InternalEList<?>)getProjects()).basicRemove(otherEnd, msgs);
    }
    return super.eInverseRemove(otherEnd, featureID, msgs);
  }

  @Override
  public NotificationChain eBasicRemoveFromContainerFeature(NotificationChain msgs)
  {
    switch (eContainerFeatureID())
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return eInternalContainer().eInverseRemove(this, ProjectPackage.COMPANY__DEPARTMENTS, Company.class, msgs);
    }
    return super.eBasicRemoveFromContainerFeature(msgs);
  }

  @Override
  public Object eGet(int featureID, boolean resolve, boolean coreType)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        return getID();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        return getEmail();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return getEmployees();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return getCompany();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return getProjects();
    }
    return super.eGet(featureID, resolve, coreType);
  }

  @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
  @Override
  public void eSet(int featureID, Object newValue)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        setID((String)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        setEmail((String)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        getEmployees().clear();
        getEmployees().addAll((Collection<? extends Employee>)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        setCompany((Company)newValue);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        getProjects().clear();
        getProjects().addAll((Collection<? extends Project>)newValue);
        return;
    }
    super.eSet(featureID, newValue);
  }

  @Override
  public void eUnset(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        setID(ID_EDEFAULT);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        setEmail(EMAIL_EDEFAULT);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        getEmployees().clear();
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        setCompany((Company)null);
        return;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        getProjects().clear();
        return;
    }
    super.eUnset(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public boolean eIsSet(int featureID)
  {
    switch (featureID)
    {
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__ID:
        return ID_EDEFAULT == null ? id != null : !ID_EDEFAULT.equals(id);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMAIL:
        return EMAIL_EDEFAULT == null ? email != null : !EMAIL_EDEFAULT.equals(email);
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__EMPLOYEES:
        return employees != null && !employees.isEmpty();
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__COMPANY:
        return getCompany() != null;
      case ProjectPackage.DEPARTMENT__PROJECTS:
        return projects != null && !projects.isEmpty();
    }
    return super.eIsSet(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString()
  {
    if (eIsProxy()) return super.toString();

    StringBuffer result = new StringBuffer(super.toString());
    result.append(" (id: ");
    result.append(id);
    result.append(", email: ");
    result.append(email);
    result.append(')');
    return result.toString();
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

+ Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects

3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
Summary: Calculates the average budget amount of all projects in the department.
Algorithm: 
  1. If projects list is empty, return 0.0.
  2. Sum the budget of all projects in this.projects.
  3. Return total / projects.size().
Related Features: projects
Related Classes: Project, Department
Input: None
Output: double, the average budget.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the average budget value.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
Summary: Retrieves the funding group types of all community projects within the department.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize List<FundingGroupType> types.
  2. For each project in this.projects:
     If project is instance of CommunityProject:
        types.add(project.fundingGroup.type).
  3. Return types.
Related Features: projects
Related Classes: Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
Input: None
Output: List<FundingGroupType>, a list of unique or non-unique funding group types.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns a list of funding group types associated with community projects.

# Operation Specifications
Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production project.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize a Set<Employee> uniqueEmployees.
  2. For each department in departments:
     For each project in department.projects:
        If project is an instance of ProductionProject:
           For each employee in project.workingEmployees:
              uniqueEmployees.add(employee).
  3. Return uniqueEmployees.size().
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input: None
Output: int, the count of distinct employees.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the count of distinct employees associated with production projects.

Company::addDepartment
Summary: Adds a new department to the company while enforcing constraints.
Algorithm: 
  1. Check if department is null, or if name is empty/duplicate. If so, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Check if current departments size >= 8. If so, throw IllegalStateException.
  3. Add department to the departments list.
  4. Set department's company reference to this.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department, Company
Input: department (Department), the department object to add.
Output: void
Pre-condition: The company must have between 2 and 7 departments inclusive before addition.
Post-condition: The department is added to the list, and company size increases by 1.

Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary: Calculates the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of the company.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize totalBudget = 0.
  2. For each department in departments:
     For each project in department.projects:
        totalBudget += project.budget.
  3. Return totalBudget.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input: None
Output: double, the sum of all project budgets.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the correct sum of budgets across all departments.

Company::removeDepartment
Summary: Removes a department by name from the company while enforcing constraints.
Algorithm: 
  1. Find department with the given name. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Check if current departments size <= 2. If so, throw IllegalStateException.
  3. Remove the department from the list.
  4. Return the name of the removed department.
Related Features: departments
Related Classes: Department, Company
Input: name (String), the name/ID of the department to remove.
Output: String, the name of the removed department.
Pre-condition: The company must have between 3 and 8 departments inclusive before removal.
Post-condition: The department is removed, and company size decreases by 1.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:

2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;

6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
```

    if (department == null || department.getID() == null || department.getID().isEmpty()) {

    for (Department d : getDepartments()) {
        if (d.getID().equals(department.getID())) {

        }
    }
    
    if (getDepartments().size() >= 8) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Maximum number of departments (8) reached.");

    getDepartments().add(department);

  public String removeDepartment(final String name)
  {
    if (name == null || name.isEmpty()) {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    
    if (getDepartments().size() <= 2) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Cannot remove department, minimum of 2 departments required.");

    Department departmentToRemove = null;
    for (Department d : getDepartments()) {
        if (name.equals(d.getID())) {

        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department with name " + name + " not found.");
    }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
